import { Tabs, TabItem } from '@astrojs/starlight/components';

This is an interesting sutta that documents the Buddha's renunciation and search for liberation from suffering. It serves the purpose of emphasising the Buddha's rejection of the practices of his teachers, and documents his own path.

The sutta ends by inserting the Buddha's experience of the four `jhānā` as the true path to awakening, followed by the destruction of the non-optimal flows (`āsavā`) and the three knowledges — this is obviously an attempt by the author to link these later concepts to the Buddha's awakening. However, this sutta is clearly older than the similar [9M/3.6 Pāsarāsisutta](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/9M/3/3.6.html), which attempts to link in even later concepts such as the formless jhānā, Māra, and the cessation of apperception and feeling processes (`saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ`).

## Introduction

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
1228\. Thus heard by me — at one time the Bhagavā was dwelling at Vesālī, in the Great Wood, in the Gabled Hall. Now at that time, in the morning, the Bhagavā, well dressed and taking his bowl and robes, intended to enter Vesālī for alms. Then Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son, wandering and walking for exercise, approached the Great Wood and the Gabled Hall. Āyasmā Ānanda saw Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son coming from afar. Having seen him, he said this to the Bhagavā:

> “Bhante, this Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son is coming, a debater, a clever speaker, well respected by the multitude. He, Bhante, desires to dispraise the Buddha, desires to dispraise the Dhamma, desires to dispraise the Saṅgha. It would be good, Bhante, if the Bhagavā would sit for a moment, out of compassion.”

The Bhagavā sat down on the prepared seat. Then Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son approached the Bhagavā; having approached, he exchanged friendly greetings with the Bhagavā, and after exchanging polite and courteous talk, he sat down to one side. Seated to one side, Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son said this to the Bhagavā:

> 1229\. “There are, Bho Gotama, some `samaṇabrāhmaṇā` (religious practitioners) who dwell devoted to the development of the body (`kāyabhāvanā`), but not the development of the mind (`cittabhāvanā`). For they experience physical painful `vedanā` (feelings). It has happened before, Bho Gotama, that when one experiences physical painful feelings, thigh-stiffness would occur, the heart would burst, hot blood would issue from the mouth, or one would go mad, attaining mental derangement. For him, Bho Gotama, the mind follows the body, it comes under the sway of the body. For what reason? Because of the undeveloped nature of the mind. But there are, Bho Gotama, some religious practitioners who dwell devoted to the development of the mind, but not the development of the body. For they experience mental painful feeling. It has happened before, Bho Gotama, that when one experiences mental painful feeling, thigh-stiffness would occur, the heart would burst, hot blood would issue from the mouth, or one would go mad, attaining mental derangement. For him, Bho Gotama, the body follows the mind, it comes under the sway of the mind. For what reason? Because of the undeveloped nature of the body. It occurs to me, Bho Gotama: ‘Surely the disciples of Bho Gotama dwell devoted to the development of the mind, but not the development of the body.’”

> 1230\. “But what have you heard, Aggivessana, as the development of the body?”

> “That is to say: Nanda Vaccha, Kisa Saṅkicca, Makkhali Gosāla — these, Bho Gotama, are naked ascetics, of loose habits, licking their hands, not coming when called, not stopping when called; they do not consent to food brought to them, nor food prepared for them, nor to an invitation. They do not accept from the mouth of a pot, nor from the mouth of a bowl, not across a threshold, not across a stick, not across a pestle, not from two eating together, not from a pregnant woman, not from a woman giving suck, not from a woman among men, not from where food is collected, not from where a dog is standing, not from where flies are swarming; they consume no fish or meat, no liquor, no wine, no fermented brew. They are one-house-men, taking one mouthful; or two-house-men, taking two mouthfuls... or seven-house-men, taking seven mouthfuls. They subsist on one offering, on two offerings... on seven offerings. They take food once a day, once every two days... once every seven days. Thus they dwell devoted to the practice of eating meals at intervals, even up to half a month.”

> 1231\. “But do they, Aggivessana, subsist on just that much?”

> “Of course not, Bho Gotama. Sometimes, Bho Gotama, they chew splendid hard foods, eat splendid soft foods, taste splendid tastes, drink splendid drinks. They give this body strength, make it grow, and fatten it.”

> 1232\. “What they give up earlier, Aggivessana, they gather later; thus there is the increase and decrease of this body. But what have you heard, Aggivessana, as the development of the mind?”

When asked by the Bhagavā about the development of the mind, Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son could not answer.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
1228\. Evaṃ me sutaṃ—  ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ. Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ sunivattho hoti pattacīvaramādāya vesāliṃ piṇḍāya pavisitukāmo. Atha kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto jaṅghāvihāraṃ anucaṅkamamāno anuvicaramāno yena mahāvanaṃ kūṭāgārasālā tenupasaṅkami. Addasā kho āyasmā ānando saccakaṃ nigaṇṭhaputtaṃ dūratova āgacchantaṃ. Disvāna bhagavantaṃ etadavoca—  “ayaṃ, bhante, saccako nigaṇṭhaputto āgacchati bhassappavādako paṇḍitavādo sādhusammato bahujanassa. Eso kho, bhante, avaṇṇakāmo buddhassa, avaṇṇakāmo dhammassa, avaṇṇakāmo saṃghassa. Sādhu, bhante, bhagavā muhuttaṃ nisīdatu anukampaṃ upādāyā”ti. Nisīdi bhagavā paññatte āsane. Atha kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavatā saddhiṃ sammodi, sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sāraṇīyaṃ vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto bhagavantaṃ etadavoca—

(365.)

1229\. “Santi, bho gotama, eke samaṇabrāhmaṇā kāyabhāvanānuyogamanuyuttā viharanti, no cittabhāvanaṃ. Phusanti hi te, bho gotama, sārīrikaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ. Bhūtapubbaṃ, bho gotama, sārīrikāya dukkhāya vedanāya phuṭṭhassa sato ūrukkhambhopi nāma bhavissati, hadayampi nāma phalissati, uṇhampi lohitaṃ mukhato uggamissati, ummādampi pāpuṇissati cittakkhepaṃ. Tassa kho etaṃ, bho gotama, kāyanvayaṃ cittaṃ hoti, kāyassa vasena vattati. Taṃ kissa hetu? Abhāvitattā cittassa. Santi pana, bho gotama, eke samaṇabrāhmaṇā cittabhāvanānuyogamanuyuttā viharanti, no kāyabhāvanaṃ. Phusanti hi te, bho gotama, cetasikaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ. Bhūtapubbaṃ, bho gotama, cetasikāya dukkhāya vedanāya phuṭṭhassa sato ūrukkhambhopi nāma bhavissati, hadayampi nāma phalissati, uṇhampi lohitaṃ mukhato uggamissati, ummādampi pāpuṇissati cittakkhepaṃ. Tassa kho eso, bho gotama, cittanvayo kāyo hoti, cittassa vasena vattati. Taṃ kissa hetu? Abhāvitattā kāyassa. Tassa mayhaṃ, bho gotama, evaṃ hoti—  ‘addhā bhoto gotamassa sāvakā cittabhāvanānuyogamanuyuttā viharanti, no kāyabhāvanan’”ti.

(366.)

1230\. “Kinti pana te, aggivessana, kāyabhāvanā sutā”ti? “Seyyathidaṃ—  nando vaccho, kiso saṅkicco, makkhali gosālo—  etehi, bho gotama, acelakā muttācārā hatthāpalekhanā naehibhaddantikā natiṭṭhabhaddantikā na abhihaṭaṃ na uddissakataṃ na nimantanaṃ sādiyanti, te na kumbhimukhā paṭiggaṇhanti na kaḷopimukhā paṭiggaṇhanti na eḷakamantaraṃ na daṇḍamantaraṃ na musalamantaraṃ na dvinnaṃ bhuñjamānānaṃ na gabbhiniyā na pāyamānāya na purisantaragatāya na saṅkittīsu na yattha sā upaṭṭhito hoti na yattha makkhikā saṇḍasaṇḍacārinī, na macchaṃ na maṃsaṃ na suraṃ na merayaṃ na thusodakaṃ pivanti. Te ekāgārikā vā honti ekālopikā, dvāgārikā vā honti dvālopikā…pe…  sattāgārikā vā honti sattālopikā. Ekissāpi dattiyā yāpenti, dvīhipi dattīhi yāpenti…pe…  sattahipi dattīhi yāpenti. Ekāhikampi āhāraṃ āhārenti, dvīhikampi āhāraṃ āhārenti…pe…  sattāhikampi āhāraṃ āhārenti. Iti evarūpaṃ addhamāsikampi pariyāyabhattabhojanānuyogamanuyuttā viharantī”ti.

1231\. “Kiṃ pana te, aggivessana, tāvatakeneva yāpentī”ti? “No hidaṃ, bho gotama. Appekadā, bho gotama, uḷārāni uḷārāni khādanīyāni khādanti, uḷārāni uḷārāni bhojanāni bhuñjanti, uḷārāni uḷārāni sāyanīyāni sāyanti, uḷārāni uḷārāni pānāni pivanti. Te imaṃ kāyaṃ balaṃ gāhenti nāma, brūhenti nāma, medenti nāmā”ti.

1232\. “Yaṃ kho te, aggivessana, purimaṃ pahāya pacchā upacinanti, evaṃ imassa kāyassa ācayāpacayo hoti. Kinti pana te, aggivessana, cittabhāvanā sutā”ti? Cittabhāvanāya kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto bhagavatā puṭṭho samāno na sampāyāsi.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
1228\. 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀫𑁂 𑀲𑀼𑀢𑀁—  𑀏𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀮𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺 𑀫𑀳𑀸𑀯𑀦𑁂 𑀓𑀽𑀝𑀸𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀲𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀬𑀁. 𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀧𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀲𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀲𑀼𑀦𑀺𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁄 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀘𑀻𑀯𑀭𑀫𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀮𑀺𑀁 𑀧𑀺𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀯𑀺𑀲𑀺𑀢𑀼𑀓𑀸𑀫𑁄. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀓𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀚𑀗𑁆𑀖𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀘𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀯𑀺𑀘𑀭𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀫𑀳𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀁 𑀓𑀽𑀝𑀸𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀲𑀸𑀮𑀸 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺. 𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑀸 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁄 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀓𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀤𑀽𑀭𑀢𑁄𑀯 𑀆𑀕𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁. 𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀦 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—  “𑀅𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀓𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀆𑀕𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀯𑀸𑀤𑀓𑁄 𑀧𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀺𑀢𑀯𑀸𑀤𑁄 𑀲𑀸𑀥𑀼𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀢𑁄 𑀩𑀳𑀼𑀚𑀦𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀏𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀯𑀡𑁆𑀡𑀓𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀩𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀲𑁆𑀲, 𑀅𑀯𑀡𑁆𑀡𑀓𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲, 𑀅𑀯𑀡𑁆𑀡𑀓𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀁𑀖𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀲𑀸𑀥𑀼, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀫𑀼𑀳𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀻𑀤𑀢𑀼 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬𑀸”𑀢𑀺. 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀻𑀤𑀺 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂 𑀆𑀲𑀦𑁂. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀓𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀺𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄𑀤𑀺, 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄𑀤𑀦𑀻𑀬𑀁 𑀓𑀣𑀁 𑀲𑀸𑀭𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀻𑀢𑀺𑀲𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀏𑀓𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀻𑀤𑀺. 𑀏𑀓𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀓𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—

1229\. “𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀏𑀓𑁂 𑀲𑀫𑀡𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀼𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀫𑀦𑀼𑀬𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀦𑁄 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀁. 𑀨𑀼𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀳𑀺 𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀲𑀸𑀭𑀻𑀭𑀺𑀓𑀁 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀁 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀁. 𑀪𑀽𑀢𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀁, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀲𑀸𑀭𑀻𑀭𑀺𑀓𑀸𑀬 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀨𑀼𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀲𑀢𑁄 𑀊𑀭𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀫𑁆𑀪𑁄𑀧𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫 𑀪𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀳𑀤𑀬𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫 𑀨𑀮𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀉𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀮𑁄𑀳𑀺𑀢𑀁 𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀢𑁄 𑀉𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀫𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀉𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸𑀤𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀼𑀡𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀧𑀁. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀔𑁄 𑀏𑀢𑀁, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀯𑀬𑀁 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺, 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀯𑀲𑁂𑀦 𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑀁 𑀓𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑀼? 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀦, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀏𑀓𑁂 𑀲𑀫𑀡𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀼𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀫𑀦𑀼𑀬𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀦𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀁. 𑀨𑀼𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀳𑀺 𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀘𑁂𑀢𑀲𑀺𑀓𑀁 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀁 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀁. 𑀪𑀽𑀢𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀁, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀘𑁂𑀢𑀲𑀺𑀓𑀸𑀬 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀨𑀼𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀲𑀢𑁄 𑀊𑀭𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀫𑁆𑀪𑁄𑀧𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫 𑀪𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀳𑀤𑀬𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫 𑀨𑀮𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀉𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀮𑁄𑀳𑀺𑀢𑀁 𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀢𑁄 𑀉𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀫𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀉𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸𑀤𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀼𑀡𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀧𑀁. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀔𑁄 𑀏𑀲𑁄, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑁆𑀯𑀬𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺, 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀯𑀲𑁂𑀦 𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑀁 𑀓𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑀼? 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺—  ‘𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸 𑀪𑁄𑀢𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀓𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀼𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀫𑀦𑀼𑀬𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀦𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀦𑁆’”𑀢𑀺.

1230\. “𑀓𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀦 𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀢𑀸”𑀢𑀺? “𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀣𑀺𑀤𑀁—  𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁄 𑀯𑀘𑁆𑀙𑁄, 𑀓𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀺𑀘𑁆𑀘𑁄, 𑀫𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀮𑀺 𑀕𑁄𑀲𑀸𑀮𑁄—  𑀏𑀢𑁂𑀳𑀺, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀅𑀘𑁂𑀮𑀓𑀸 𑀫𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀸 𑀳𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀮𑁂𑀔𑀦𑀸 𑀦𑀏𑀳𑀺𑀪𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀓𑀸 𑀦𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀪𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀓𑀸 𑀦 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀳𑀝𑀁 𑀦 𑀉𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀓𑀢𑀁 𑀦 𑀦𑀺𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀲𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀢𑁂 𑀦 𑀓𑀼𑀫𑁆𑀪𑀺𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀦 𑀓𑀍𑀑𑀧𑀺𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀦 𑀏𑀍𑀅𑀓𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀦 𑀤𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀦 𑀫𑀼𑀲𑀮𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀦 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀁 𑀪𑀼𑀜𑁆𑀚𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀦 𑀕𑀩𑁆𑀪𑀺𑀦𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀦 𑀧𑀸𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀧𑀼𑀭𑀺𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀕𑀢𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀻𑀲𑀼 𑀦 𑀬𑀢𑁆𑀣 𑀲𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀦 𑀬𑀢𑁆𑀣 𑀫𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀺𑀓𑀸 𑀲𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀲𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀦𑀻, 𑀦 𑀫𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀁 𑀦 𑀫𑀁𑀲𑀁 𑀦 𑀲𑀼𑀭𑀁 𑀦 𑀫𑁂𑀭𑀬𑀁 𑀦 𑀣𑀼𑀲𑁄𑀤𑀓𑀁 𑀧𑀺𑀯𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑁂 𑀏𑀓𑀸𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀓𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀳𑁄𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀏𑀓𑀸𑀮𑁄𑀧𑀺𑀓𑀸, 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀓𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀳𑁄𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀮𑁄𑀧𑀺𑀓𑀸…𑀧𑁂…  𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀓𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀳𑁄𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀮𑁄𑀧𑀺𑀓𑀸. 𑀏𑀓𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀤𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀬𑀸𑀧𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀻𑀳𑀺𑀧𑀺 𑀤𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀻𑀳𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀧𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺…𑀧𑁂…  𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀳𑀺𑀧𑀺 𑀤𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀻𑀳𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀧𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺. 𑀏𑀓𑀸𑀳𑀺𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀆𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀆𑀳𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀻𑀳𑀺𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀆𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀆𑀳𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺…𑀧𑁂…  𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀳𑀺𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀆𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀆𑀳𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀏𑀯𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀁 𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀫𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀬𑀪𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑁄𑀚𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀼𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀫𑀦𑀼𑀬𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀻”𑀢𑀺.

1231\. “𑀓𑀺𑀁 𑀧𑀦 𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀓𑁂𑀦𑁂𑀯 𑀬𑀸𑀧𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀻”𑀢𑀺? “𑀦𑁄 𑀳𑀺𑀤𑀁, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫. 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀓𑀤𑀸, 𑀪𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫, 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀔𑀸𑀤𑀦𑀻𑀬𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀔𑀸𑀤𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀪𑁄𑀚𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀪𑀼𑀜𑁆𑀚𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀲𑀸𑀬𑀦𑀻𑀬𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀲𑀸𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀧𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀧𑀺𑀯𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑁂 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀩𑀮𑀁 𑀕𑀸𑀳𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫, 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀽𑀳𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫, 𑀫𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀸”𑀢𑀺.

1232\. “𑀬𑀁 𑀔𑁄 𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀧𑀼𑀭𑀺𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀳𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀘𑀺𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀇𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀆𑀘𑀬𑀸𑀧𑀘𑀬𑁄 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺. 𑀓𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀦 𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀢𑀸”𑀢𑀺? 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀔𑁄 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀓𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀝𑁆𑀞𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀸𑀬𑀸𑀲𑀺.
</TabItem>
<TabItem label="Interlinear">
1228.1 Evaṃ me sutaṃ—  ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā vesāliyaṃ viharati mahāvane kūṭāgārasālāyaṃ.  
⏏️(evaṃ) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(ahaṃ) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(suta) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(eka) 🚹⨀②(samaya) 🚹⨀①(bhagavant) 🚺⨀⑦(vesālī) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(viharati) 🚻⨀⑦🆎(mahā+vana) 🚺⨀⑦🆎(kūṭa+agāra+sālā)  
thus | by me | heard | one | time | the Bhagavā | at Vesālī | dwells | in the Great Wood | in the Gabled Hall  
Thus heard by me — at one time the Bhagavā was dwelling at Vesālī, in the Great Wood, in the Gabled Hall.

1228.2 Tena kho pana samayena bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ sunivattho hoti pattacīvaramādāya vesāliṃ piṇḍāya pavisitukāmo.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ta) ⏏️(kho) ⏏️(pana) 🚹⨀③(samaya) 🚹⨀①(bhagavant) 🚹⨀②🆎(pubbaṇha+samaya) 🚹⨀①🆎(su+nivattha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) 🚻⨀②🆎(patta+cīvara) 🔽(ya)(ādāti) 🚺⨀②(vesālī) 🚹⨀④(piṇḍa) 🚹⨀①🆎(pavisituṃ+kāma)  
at that | indeed | now | time | the Bhagavā | in the morning | well dressed | is | bowl and robes | taking | Vesālī | for alms | wanting to enter  
Now at that time, in the morning, the Bhagavā, well dressed and taking his bowl and robes, intended to enter Vesālī for alms.

1228.3 Atha kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto jaṅghāvihāraṃ anucaṅkamamāno anuvicaramāno yena mahāvanaṃ kūṭāgārasālā tenupasaṅkami.  
⏏️(atha) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀①(saccaka) 🚹⨀①🆎(nigaṇṭha+putta) 🚹⨀②🆎(jaṅghā+vihāra) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(anucaṅkamamāna) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(anuvicaramāna) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ya) 🚻⨀①🆎(mahā+vana) 🚺⨀①🆎(kūṭa+agāra+sālā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(upasaṅkamati)  
then | indeed | Saccaka | the Nigaṇṭha’s son | for exercise | walking | wandering | by where | the Great Wood | the Gabled Hall | by there | approached  
Then Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son, wandering and walking for exercise, approached the Great Wood and the Gabled Hall.

1228.4 Addasā kho āyasmā ānando saccakaṃ nigaṇṭhaputtaṃ dūratova āgacchantaṃ.  
🟢⨀🤟⏮(passati) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀①(āyasmant) 🚹⨀①(ānanda) 🚹⨀②(saccaka) 🚹⨀②🆎(nigaṇṭha+putta) 🚻⨀⑤(dūra) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀②🔽(anta)(āgacchanta)  
saw | indeed | Venerable | Ānanda | Saccaka | the Nigaṇṭha’s son | from afar | just | coming  
Āyasmā Ānanda saw Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son coming from afar.

1228.5 Disvāna bhagavantaṃ etadavoca—  “ayaṃ, bhante, saccako nigaṇṭhaputto āgacchati bhassappavādako paṇḍitavādo sādhusammato bahujanassa.  
🔽(tvāna)(passati) 🚹⨀②(bhagavant) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(vadati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ima) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀①(saccaka) 🚹⨀①🆎(nigaṇṭha+putta) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(āgacchati) 🚹⨀①🆎(bhassa+pavādaka) 🚹⨀①🆎(paṇḍita+vāda) 🚹⨀①🆎(sādhu+sammata) 🚹⨀④🆎(bahu+jana)  
having seen | to the Bhagavā | this | said | this | Bhante | Saccaka | the Nigaṇṭha’s son | is coming | a debater | a clever speaker | well respected | by the multitude  
Having seen him, he said this to the Bhagavā: “Bhante, this Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son is coming, a debater, a clever speaker, well respected by the multitude.

1228.6 Eso kho, bhante, avaṇṇakāmo buddhassa, avaṇṇakāmo dhammassa, avaṇṇakāmo saṃghassa.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(eta) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+vaṇṇa+kāma) 🚹⨀⑥(buddha) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+vaṇṇa+kāma) 🚹⨀⑥(dhamma) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+vaṇṇa+kāma) 🚹⨀⑥(saṅgha)  
he | indeed | Bhante | desiring to dispraise | of the Buddha | desiring to dispraise | of the Dhamma | desiring to dispraise | of the Saṅgha  
He, Bhante, desires to dispraise the Buddha, desires to dispraise the Dhamma, desires to dispraise the Saṅgha.

1228.7 Sādhu, bhante, bhagavā muhuttaṃ nisīdatu anukampaṃ upādāyā”ti.  
⏏️(sādhu) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀①(bhagavant) 🚹⨀②(muhutta) 🟢⨀🤟⏹(nisīdati) 🚺⨀②(anukampā) ⏏️(upādāya) ⏏️(iti)  
it would be good | Bhante | the Bhagavā | for a moment | may sit | compassion | out of | (end quote)  
It would be good, Bhante, if the Bhagavā would sit for a moment, out of compassion.”

1228.8 Nisīdi bhagavā paññatte āsane.  
🟢⨀🤟⏮(nisīdati) 🚹⨀①(bhagavant) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(ta)(paññatta) 🚻⨀⑦(āsana)  
sat down | the Bhagavā | prepared | on the seat  
The Bhagavā sat down on the prepared seat.

1228.9 Atha kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami;  
⏏️(atha) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀①(saccaka) 🚹⨀①🆎(nigaṇṭha+putta) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ya) 🚹⨀①(bhagavant) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(upasaṅkamati)  
then | indeed | Saccaka | the Nigaṇṭha’s son | by where | the Bhagavā | by there | approached  
Then Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son approached the Bhagavā;

1228.10 upasaṅkamitvā bhagavatā saddhiṃ sammodi, sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sāraṇīyaṃ vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi.  
🔽(tvā)(upasaṅkamati) 🚹⨀③(bhagavant) ⏏️(saddhiṃ) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(sammodati) 🚺⨀②🔽(anīya)(sammodanīya) 🚺⨀②(kathā) 🚺⨀②🔽(anīya)(sāraṇīya) 🔽(tvā)(vītisāreti) ⏏️🆎(eka+anta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(nisīdati)  
having approached | with the Bhagavā | together | exchanged greetings | polite | talk | courteous | having concluded | to one side | sat down  
having approached, he exchanged friendly greetings with the Bhagavā, and after exchanging polite and courteous talk, he sat down to one side.

1228.11 Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto bhagavantaṃ etadavoca—  
⏏️🆎(eka+anta) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(nisinna) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀①(saccaka) 🚹⨀①🆎(nigaṇṭha+putta) 🚹⨀②(bhagavant) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(vadati)  
to one side | seated | indeed | Saccaka | the Nigaṇṭha’s son | to the Bhagavā | this | said  
Seated to one side, Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son said this to the Bhagavā:

1229.1 “Santi, bho gotama, eke samaṇabrāhmaṇā kāyabhāvanānuyogamanuyuttā viharanti, no cittabhāvanaṃ.  
🟢⨂🤟▶️(atthi) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(eka) 🚹⨂①🆎(samaṇa+brāhmaṇa) 🚹⨂①🆎(kāya+bhāvanā+anuyoga+anuyutta) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(viharati) ⏏️(no) 🚺⨀②🆎(citta+bhāvanā)  
there are | Bho | Gotama | some | religious practitioners | devoted to the development of the body | dwell | not | the development of the mind  
“There are, Bho Gotama, some `samaṇabrāhmaṇā` (religious practitioners) who dwell devoted to the development of the body (`kāyabhāvanā`), but not the development of the mind (`cittabhāvanā`).

1229.2 Phusanti hi te, bho gotama, sārīrikaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ.  
🟢⨂🤟▶️(phusati) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ta) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) 🚺⨀②(sārīrika) 🚺⨀②(dukkha) 🚺⨀②(vedanā)  
experience | for | they | Bho | Gotama | physical | painful | feeling  
For they experience physical painful `vedanā` (feelings).

1229.3 Bhūtapubbaṃ, bho gotama, sārīrikāya dukkhāya vedanāya phuṭṭhassa sato ūrukkhambhopi nāma bhavissati, hadayampi nāma phalissati, uṇhampi lohitaṃ mukhato uggamissati, ummādampi pāpuṇissati cittakkhepaṃ.  
🚻⨀②🆎(bhūta+pubba) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) 🚺⨀③(sārīrika) 🚺⨀③(dukkha) 🚺⨀③(vedanā) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(phuṭṭha) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(anta)(santa) 🚹⨀①🆎(ūru+khambha) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(nāma) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚻⨀①(hadaya) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(nāma) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(phalati) 🚻⨀①(uṇha) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨀①(lohita) 🚻⨀⑤(mukha) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(uggacchati) 🚹⨀②(ummāda) ⏏️(api) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(pāpuṇāti) 🚹⨀②🆎(citta+khepa)  
formerly | Bho | Gotama | by a physical | painful | feeling | of one touched | being | thigh-stiffness | even | indeed | will occur | the heart | even | indeed | will burst | hot | even | blood | from the mouth | will issue | madness | even | will attain | mental derangement  
It has happened before, Bho Gotama, that when one experiences physical painful feelings, thigh-stiffness would occur, the heart would burst, hot blood would issue from the mouth, or one would go mad, attaining mental derangement.

1229.4 Tassa kho etaṃ, bho gotama, kāyanvayaṃ cittaṃ hoti, kāyassa vasena vattati.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(ta) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(eta) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) 🚻⨀①🆎(kāya+anvaya) 🚻⨀①(citta) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) 🚹⨀⑥(kāya) 🚹⨀③(vasa) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(vattati)  
his | indeed | this | Bho | Gotama | following the body | mind | is | of the body | under the sway | proceeds  
For him, Bho Gotama, the mind follows the body, it comes under the sway of the body.

1229.5 Taṃ kissa hetu?  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀⑥(kiṃ) 🚹⨀①(hetu)  
that | of what | the reason  
For what reason?

1229.6 Abhāvitattā cittassa.  
🚻⨀⑤🔽(tta)🆎(na+bhāvitatta) 🚻⨀⑥(citta)  
because of the undeveloped nature | of the mind  
Because of the undeveloped nature of the mind.

1229.7 Santi pana, bho gotama, eke samaṇabrāhmaṇā cittabhāvanānuyogamanuyuttā viharanti, no kāyabhāvanaṃ.  
🟢⨂🤟▶️(atthi) ⏏️(pana) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(eka) 🚹⨂①🆎(samaṇa+brāhmaṇa) 🚹⨂①🆎(citta+bhāvanā+anuyoga+anuyutta) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(viharati) ⏏️(no) 🚺⨀②🆎(kāya+bhāvanā)  
there are | but | Bho | Gotama | some | religious practitioners | devoted to the development of the mind | dwell | not | the development of the body  
But there are, Bho Gotama, some religious practitioners who dwell devoted to the development of the mind, but not the development of the body.

1229.8 Phusanti hi te, bho gotama, cetasikaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ.  
🟢⨂🤟▶️(phusati) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ta) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) 🚺⨀②(cetasika) 🚺⨀②(dukkha) 🚺⨀②(vedanā)  
experience | for | they | Bho | Gotama | mental | painful | feeling  
For they experience mental painful feeling.

1229.9 Bhūtapubbaṃ, bho gotama, cetasikāya dukkhāya vedanāya phuṭṭhassa sato ūrukkhambhopi nāma bhavissati, hadayampi nāma phalissati, uṇhampi lohitaṃ mukhato uggamissati, ummādampi pāpuṇissati cittakkhepaṃ.  
🚻⨀②🆎(bhūta+pubba) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) 🚺⨀③(cetasika) 🚺⨀③(dukkha) 🚺⨀③(vedanā) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(phuṭṭha) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(anta)(santa) 🚹⨀①🆎(ūru+khambha) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(nāma) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚻⨀①(hadaya) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(nāma) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(phalati) 🚻⨀①(uṇha) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨀①(lohita) 🚻⨀⑤(mukha) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(uggacchati) 🚹⨀②(ummāda) ⏏️(api) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(pāpuṇāti) 🚹⨀②🆎(citta+khepa)  
formerly | Bho | Gotama | by a mental | painful | feeling | of one touched | being | thigh-stiffness | even | indeed | will occur | the heart | even | indeed | will burst | hot | even | blood | from the mouth | will issue | madness | even | will attain | mental derangement  
It has happened before, Bho Gotama, that when one experiences mental painful feeling, thigh-stiffness would occur, the heart would burst, hot blood would issue from the mouth, or one would go mad, attaining mental derangement.

1229.10 Tassa kho eso, bho gotama, cittanvayo kāyo hoti, cittassa vasena vattati.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(ta) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(eta) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) 🚹⨀①🆎(citta+anvaya) 🚹⨀①(kāya) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) 🚻⨀⑥(citta) 🚹⨀③(vasa) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(vattati)  
his | indeed | this | Bho | Gotama | following the mind | body | is | of the mind | under the sway | proceeds  
For him, Bho Gotama, the body follows the mind, it comes under the sway of the mind.

1229.11 Taṃ kissa hetu?  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀⑥(kiṃ) 🚹⨀①(hetu)  
that | of what | the reason  
For what reason?

1229.12 Abhāvitattā kāyassa.  
🚻⨀⑤🔽(tta)🆎(na+bhāvitatta) 🚹⨀⑥(kāya)  
because of the undeveloped nature | of the body  
Because of the undeveloped nature of the body.

1229.13 Tassa mayhaṃ, bho gotama, evaṃ hoti—  ‘addhā bhoto gotamassa sāvakā cittabhāvanānuyogamanuyuttā viharanti, no kāyabhāvanan’”ti.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀④(ta) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) 🚹⨀⓪(gotama) ⏏️(evaṃ) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) ⏏️(addhā) 🚹⨀⑥(bhavant) 🚹⨀⑥(gotama) 🚹⨂①(sāvaka) 🚹⨂①🆎(citta+bhāvanā+anuyoga+anuyutta) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(viharati) ⏏️(no) 🚺⨀②🆎(kāya+bhāvanā) ⏏️(iti)  
to that | to me | Bho | Gotama | thus | it occurs | surely | of Bho | Gotama | disciples | devoted to the development of the mind | dwell | not | the development of the body | (end quote)  
It occurs to me, Bho Gotama: ‘Surely the disciples of Bho Gotama dwell devoted to the development of the mind, but not the development of the body.’”

1230.1 “Kinti pana te, aggivessana, kāyabhāvanā sutā”ti?  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kiṃ) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(pana) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀⑥(tvaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚺⨀①🆎(kāya+bhāvanā) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(suta) ⏏️(iti)  
what | as | but | by you | Aggivessana | the development of the body | heard | (end quote)  
“But what have you heard, Aggivessana, as the development of the body?”

1230.2 “Seyyathidaṃ—  nando vaccho, kiso saṅkicco, makkhali gosālo—  etehi, bho gotama, acelakā muttācārā hatthāpalekhanā naehibhaddantikā natiṭṭhabhaddantikā na abhihaṭaṃ na uddissakataṃ na nimantanaṃ sādiyanti, te na kumbhimukhā paṭiggaṇhanti na kaḷopimukhā paṭiggaṇhanti na eḷakamantaraṃ na daṇḍamantaraṃ na musalamantaraṃ na dvinnaṃ bhuñjamānānaṃ na gabbhiniyā na pāyamānāya na purisantaragatāya na saṅkittīsu na yattha sā upaṭṭhito hoti na yattha makkhikā saṇḍasaṇḍacārinī, na macchaṃ na maṃsaṃ na suraṃ na merayaṃ na thusodakaṃ pivanti.

that is to say | Nanda | Vaccha | Kisa | Saṅkicca | Makkhali | Gosāla | these | indeed | Bho | Gotama | naked ascetics | of loose habits | licking their hands | not coming when called | not stopping when called | not | food brought | not | food prepared for them | not | an invitation | consent to | they | not | from the mouth of a pot | accept | not | from the mouth of a bowl | accept | not | across a threshold | not | across a stick | not | across a pestle | not | of two | eating together | not | from a pregnant woman | not | from a woman giving suck | not | from a woman among men | not | where food is collected | not | where | a dog | standing | is | not | where | flies | swarming in swarms | not | fish | not | meat | not | liquor | not | wine | not | sour gruel | drink  
“That is to say: Nanda Vaccha, Kisa Saṅkicca, Makkhali Gosāla — these, Bho Gotama, are naked ascetics, of loose habits, licking their hands, not coming when called, not stopping when called; they do not consent to food brought to them, nor food prepared for them, nor to an invitation. They do not accept from the mouth of a pot, nor from the mouth of a bowl, not across a threshold, not across a stick, not across a pestle, not from two eating together, not from a pregnant woman, not from a woman giving suck, not from a woman among men, not from where food is collected, not from where a dog is standing, not from where flies are swarming; they consume no fish or meat, no liquor, no wine, no fermented brew.

1230.3 Te ekāgārikā vā honti ekālopikā, dvāgārikā vā honti dvālopikā…pe…  sattāgārikā vā honti sattālopikā.

They take food once a day, once every two days... once every seven days.

1230.6 Iti evarūpaṃ addhamāsikampi pariyāyabhattabhojanānuyogamanuyuttā viharantī”ti.  
⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀②🆎(evaṃ+rūpa) 🚹⨀②🆎(addha+māsika) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨂①🆎(pariyāya+bhatta+bhojana+anuyoga+anuyutta) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(viharati) ⏏️(iti)

Thus they dwell devoted to the practice of eating meals at intervals, even up to half a month.”

1231.1 “Kiṃ pana te, aggivessana, tāvatakeneva yāpentī”ti?

“Of course not, Bho Gotama.

1231.4 Te imaṃ kāyaṃ balaṃ gāhenti nāma, brūhenti nāma, medenti nāmā”ti.

“What they give up earlier, Aggivessana, they gather later; thus there is the increase and decrease of this body.

1232.2 Kinti pana te, aggivessana, cittabhāvanā sutā”ti?

about the development of the mind | indeed | Saccaka | the Nigaṇṭha’s son | by the Bhagavā | asked | being | not | could answer

## An undeveloped person

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
1233\. Then the Bhagavā said this to Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son:

> “That which you spoke of earlier, Aggivessana, as the development of the body, that is not the rightful development of the body in the Discipline of the Liberated One. You did not even know the development of the body, Aggivessana; how then will you know the development of the mind? However, Aggivessana, listen to how one is undeveloped in body and undeveloped in mind, and how one is developed in body and developed in mind. Attend closely, I shall speak.”

“Yes, bho,” Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son replied to the Bhagavā. The Bhagavā said this:

> 1234\. “And how, Aggivessana, is one undeveloped in body and undeveloped in mind? Here, Aggivessana, pleasant feeling arises in an unlearned ordinary person. Having experienced pleasant feeling, he becomes lustful for pleasure and falls into lust for pleasure. That pleasant feeling of his ceases. With the cessation of pleasant feeling, painful feeling arises. Having experienced painful feeling, he sorrows, becomes weary, laments, wails beating his breast, and falls into confusion. For him, Aggivessana, that pleasant feeling that has arisen invades his mind and remains because of the undeveloped nature of the body; and that painful feeling that has arisen invades his mind and remains because of the undeveloped nature of the mind. For anyone, Aggivessana, in whom, in this double manner, arisen pleasant feeling invades the mind and remains because of the undeveloped nature of the body, and arisen painful feeling invades the mind and remains because of the undeveloped nature of the mind — thus, Aggivessana, is one undeveloped in body and undeveloped in mind.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
1233\. Atha kho bhagavā saccakaṃ nigaṇṭhaputtaṃ etadavoca—  “yāpi kho te esā, aggivessana, purimā kāyabhāvanā bhāsitā sāpi ariyassa vinaye no dhammikā kāyabhāvanā. Kāyabhāvanampi kho tvaṃ, aggivessana, na aññāsi, kuto pana tvaṃ cittabhāvanaṃ jānissasi? Api ca, aggivessana, yathā abhāvitakāyo ca hoti abhāvitacitto ca, bhāvitakāyo ca hoti bhāvitacitto ca. Taṃ suṇāhi, sādhukaṃ manasi karohi, bhāsissāmī”ti. “Evaṃ, bho”ti kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto bhagavato paccassosi. Bhagavā etadavoca—

1234\. “Kathañca, aggivessana, abhāvitakāyo ca hoti abhāvitacitto ca? Idha, aggivessana, assutavato puthujjanassa uppajjati sukhā vedanā. So sukhāya vedanāya phuṭṭho samāno sukhasārāgī ca hoti sukhasārāgitañca āpajjati. Tassa sā sukhā vedanā nirujjhati. Sukhāya vedanāya nirodhā uppajjati dukkhā vedanā. So dukkhāya vedanāya phuṭṭho samāno socati kilamati paridevati urattāḷiṃ kandati sammohaṃ āpajjati. Tassa kho esā, aggivessana, uppannāpi sukhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati abhāvitattā kāyassa, uppannāpi dukkhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati abhāvitattā cittassa. Yassa kassaci, aggivessana, evaṃ ubhatopakkhaṃ uppannāpi sukhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati abhāvitattā kāyassa, uppannāpi dukkhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati abhāvitattā cittassa, evaṃ kho, aggivessana, abhāvitakāyo ca hoti abhāvitacitto ca.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
  1233\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀓𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—  “𑀬𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀔𑁄 𑀢𑁂 𑀏𑀲𑀸, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀧𑀼𑀭𑀺𑀫𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀸 𑀪𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀢𑀸 𑀲𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀅𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀯𑀺𑀦𑀬𑁂 𑀦𑁄 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀺𑀓𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀸. 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀔𑁄 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀦 𑀅𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀲𑀺, 𑀓𑀼𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀦 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀦𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀲𑀺? 𑀅𑀧𑀺 𑀘, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀬𑀣𑀸 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀘, 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀘. 𑀢𑀁 𑀲𑀼𑀡𑀸𑀳𑀺, 𑀲𑀸𑀥𑀼𑀓𑀁 𑀫𑀦𑀲𑀺 𑀓𑀭𑁄𑀳𑀺, 𑀪𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀻”𑀢𑀺. “𑀏𑀯𑀁, 𑀪𑁄”𑀢𑀺 𑀔𑁄 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀓𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀡𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁄𑀲𑀺. 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—
  
  1234\. “𑀓𑀣𑀜𑁆𑀘, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀘? 𑀇𑀥, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀢𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀼𑀣𑀼𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀦𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸. 𑀲𑁄 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀨𑀼𑀝𑁆𑀞𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀲𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀻 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀲𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀺𑀢𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀲𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀦𑀺𑀭𑀼𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀦𑀺𑀭𑁄𑀥𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸. 𑀲𑁄 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀨𑀼𑀝𑁆𑀞𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑁄𑀘𑀢𑀺 𑀓𑀺𑀮𑀫𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀤𑁂𑀯𑀢𑀺 𑀉𑀭𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀍𑀇𑀁 𑀓𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀢𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄𑀳𑀁 𑀆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀔𑁄 𑀏𑀲𑀸, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲, 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀘𑀺, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀉𑀪𑀢𑁄𑀧𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲, 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲, 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀔𑁄, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀅𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀘.
</TabItem>
<TabItem label="Interlinear">
1233.1 Atha kho bhagavā saccakaṃ nigaṇṭhaputtaṃ etadavoca—  “yāpi kho te esā, aggivessana, purimā kāyabhāvanā bhāsitā sāpi ariyassa vinaye no dhammikā kāyabhāvanā.  
⏏️(atha) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀①(bhagavant) 🚹⨀②(saccaka) 🚹⨀②🆎(nigaṇṭha+putta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(vadati) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(ya) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀⑥(tvaṃ) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(eta) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚺⨀①(purima) 🚺⨀①🆎(kāya+bhāvanā) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(bhāsita) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(ta) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨀⑥(ariya) 🚹⨀⑦(vinaya) ⏏️(no) 🚺⨀①(dhammika) 🚺⨀①🆎(kāya+bhāvanā)  
then | indeed | the Bhagavā | to Saccaka | the Nigaṇṭha’s son | this | said | which | even | indeed | by you | this | Aggivessana | earlier | development of the body | spoken of | that | even | of the liberated | in the Discipline | not | rightful | development of the body  
Then the Bhagavā said this to Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son: “That which you spoke of earlier, Aggivessana, as the development of the body, that is not the rightful development of the body in the Discipline of the Liberated One.

1233.2 Kāyabhāvanampi kho tvaṃ, aggivessana, na aññāsi, kuto pana tvaṃ cittabhāvanaṃ jānissasi?  
🚺⨀②🆎(kāya+bhāvanā) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(tvaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤘⏮(jānāti) ⏏️(kuto) ⏏️(pana) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(tvaṃ) 🚺⨀②🆎(citta+bhāvanā) 🟢⨀🤘⏭(jānāti)  
the development of the body | even | indeed | you | Aggivessana | not | knew | how | then | you | the development of the mind | will know  
You did not even know the development of the body, Aggivessana; how then will you know the development of the mind?

1233.3 Api ca, aggivessana, yathā abhāvitakāyo ca hoti abhāvitacitto ca, bhāvitakāyo ca hoti bhāvitacitto ca.  
⏏️(api) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) ⏏️(yathā) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+bhāvita+kāya) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+bhāvita+citta) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🆎(bhāvita+kāya) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) 🚹⨀①🆎(bhāvita+citta) ⏏️(ca)  
however | and | Aggivessana | how | undeveloped in body | and | one is | undeveloped in mind | and | developed in body | and | one is | developed in mind | and  
However, Aggivessana, listen to how one is undeveloped in body and undeveloped in mind, and how one is developed in body and developed in mind.

1233.4 Taṃ suṇāhi, sādhukaṃ manasi karohi, bhāsissāmī”ti.  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(ta) 🟢⨀🤘⏹(suṇāti) ⏏️(sādhukaṃ) 🚹⨀⑦(mano) 🟢⨀🤘⏹(karoti) 🟢⨀👆⏭(bhāsati) ⏏️(iti)  
that | listen | closely | in mind | make | I shall speak | (end quote)  
Attend closely, I shall speak.”

1233.5 “Evaṃ, bho”ti kho saccako nigaṇṭhaputto bhagavato paccassosi.  
⏏️(evaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(bhavant) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀①(saccaka) 🚹⨀①🆎(nigaṇṭha+putta) 🚹⨀④(bhagavant) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(paṭissuṇāti)  
yes | Bho | (end quote) | indeed | Saccaka | the Nigaṇṭha’s son | to the Bhagavā | replied  
“Yes, Bho,” Saccaka the Nigaṇṭha’s son replied to the Bhagavā.

1233.6 Bhagavā etadavoca—  
🚹⨀①(bhagavant) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(vadati)  
the Bhagavā | this | said  
The Bhagavā said this:

1234.1 “Kathañca, aggivessana, abhāvitakāyo ca hoti abhāvitacitto ca?  
⏏️(kathaṃ) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+bhāvita+kāya) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+bhāvita+citta) ⏏️(ca)  
how | and | Aggivessana | undeveloped in body | and | is | undeveloped in mind | and  
“And how, Aggivessana, is one undeveloped in body and undeveloped in mind?

1234.2 Idha, aggivessana, assutavato puthujjanassa uppajjati sukhā vedanā.  
⏏️(idha) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(na+sutavant) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(puthu+jana) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(uppajjati) 🚺⨀①(sukha) 🚺⨀①(vedanā)  
here | Aggivessana | in an unlearned | ordinary person | arises | pleasant | feeling  
Here, Aggivessana, pleasant feeling arises in an unlearned ordinary person.

1234.3 So sukhāya vedanāya phuṭṭho samāno sukhasārāgī ca hoti sukhasārāgitañca āpajjati.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) 🚺⨀③(sukha) 🚺⨀③(vedanā) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(phuṭṭha) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(samāna) 🚹⨀①🆎(sukha+sārāgī) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) 🚺⨀②🆎(sukha+sārāgitā) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(āpajjati)  
he | by pleasant | feeling | touched | being | lustful for pleasure | and | becomes | lust for pleasure | and | falls into  
Having experienced pleasant feeling, he becomes lustful for pleasure and falls into lust for pleasure.

1234.4 Tassa sā sukhā vedanā nirujjhati.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(ta) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(ta) 🚺⨀①(sukha) 🚺⨀①(vedanā) 🔴⨀🤟▶️(nirujjhati)  
of his | that | pleasant | feeling | ceases  
That pleasant feeling of his ceases.

1234.5 Sukhāya vedanāya nirodhā uppajjati dukkhā vedanā.  
🚺⨀⑥(sukha) 🚺⨀⑥(vedanā) 🚹⨀⑤(nirodha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(uppajjati) 🚺⨀①(dukkha) 🚺⨀①(vedanā)  
of pleasant | feeling | with the cessation | arises | painful | feeling  
With the cessation of pleasant feeling, painful feeling arises.

1234.6 So dukkhāya vedanāya phuṭṭho samāno socati kilamati paridevati urattāḷiṃ kandati sammohaṃ āpajjati.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) 🚺⨀③(dukkha) 🚺⨀③(vedanā) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(phuṭṭha) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(samāna) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(socati) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(kilamati) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(paridevati) ⏏️(urattāḷiṃ) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(kandati) 🚹⨀②(sammoha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(āpajjati)  
he | by painful | feeling | touched | being | sorrows | becomes weary | laments | beating his breast | wails | confusion | falls into  
Having experienced painful feeling, he sorrows, becomes weary, laments, wails beating his breast, and falls into confusion.

1234.7 Tassa kho esā, aggivessana, uppannāpi sukhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati abhāvitattā kāyassa, uppannāpi dukkhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati abhāvitattā cittassa.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(ta) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(eta) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(uppanna) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀①(sukha) 🚺⨀①(vedanā) 🚻⨀②(citta) 🔽(ya)(pariyādiyati) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṭṭhati) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(tta)🆎(na+bhāvitatta) 🚹⨀⑥(kāya) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(uppanna) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀①(dukkha) 🚺⨀①(vedanā) 🚻⨀②(citta) 🔽(ya)(pariyādiyati) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṭṭhati) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(tta)🆎(na+bhāvitatta) 🚻⨀⑥(citta)  
for him | indeed | that | Aggivessana | arisen | even | pleasant | feeling | the mind | invading | remains | because of the undeveloped nature | of the body | arisen | even | painful | feeling | the mind | invading | remains | because of the undeveloped nature | of the mind  
For him, Aggivessana, that pleasant feeling that has arisen invades his mind and remains because of the undeveloped nature of the body; and that painful feeling that has arisen invades his mind and remains because of the undeveloped nature of the mind.

1234.8 Yassa kassaci, aggivessana, evaṃ ubhatopakkhaṃ uppannāpi sukhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati abhāvitattā kāyassa, uppannāpi dukkhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati abhāvitattā cittassa, evaṃ kho, aggivessana, abhāvitakāyo ca hoti abhāvitacitto ca.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(ya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kaci) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) ⏏️(evaṃ) 🚻⨀②🆎(ubhato+pakkha) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(uppanna) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀①(sukha) 🚺⨀①(vedanā) 🚻⨀②(citta) 🔽(ya)(pariyādiyati) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṭṭhati) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(tta)🆎(na+bhāvitatta) 🚹⨀⑥(kāya) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(uppanna) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀①(dukkha) 🚺⨀①(vedanā) 🚻⨀②(citta) 🔽(ya)(pariyādiyati) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṭṭhati) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(tta)🆎(na+bhāvitatta) 🚻⨀⑥(citta) ⏏️(evaṃ) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+bhāvita+kāya) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(hoti) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+bhāvita+citta) ⏏️(ca)  
for whom | for anyone | Aggivessana | thus | in this double manner | arisen | even | pleasant | feeling | the mind | invading | remains | because of the undeveloped nature | of the body | arisen | even | painful | feeling | the mind | invading | remains | because of the undeveloped nature | of the mind | thus | indeed | Aggivessana | undeveloped in body | and | is | undeveloped in mind | and  
For anyone, Aggivessana, in whom, in this double manner, arisen pleasant feeling invades the mind and remains because of the undeveloped nature of the body, and arisen painful feeling invades the mind and remains because of the undeveloped nature of the mind — thus, Aggivessana, is one undeveloped in body and undeveloped in mind.

## A developed person

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
> 1235\. And how, Aggivessana, is one developed in body and developed in mind? Here, Aggivessana, pleasant feeling arises in a learned `ariyasāvaka` (disciple of the liberated). Having experienced pleasant feeling, he does not become lustful for pleasure, and does not fall into lust for pleasure. That pleasant feeling of his ceases. With the cessation of pleasant feeling, painful feeling arises. Having experienced painful feeling, he does not sorrow, does not become weary, does not lament, does not wail beating his breast, and does not fall into confusion. For him, Aggivessana, that pleasant feeling that has arisen does not invade his mind and remain because of the developed nature of the body; and that painful feeling that has arisen does not invade his mind and remain because of the developed nature of the mind. For anyone, Aggivessana, in whom, in this double manner, arisen pleasant feeling does not invade the mind and remain because of the developed nature of the body, and arisen painful feeling does not invade the mind and remain because of the developed nature of the mind — thus, Aggivessana, is one developed in body and developed in mind.”

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
1235\. Kathañca, aggivessana, bhāvitakāyo ca hoti bhāvitacitto ca? Idha, aggivessana, sutavato ariyasāvakassa uppajjati sukhā vedanā. So sukhāya vedanāya phuṭṭho samāno na sukhasārāgī ca hoti, na sukhasārāgitañca āpajjati. Tassa sā sukhā vedanā nirujjhati. Sukhāya vedanāya nirodhā uppajjati dukkhā vedanā. So dukkhāya vedanāya phuṭṭho samāno na socati na kilamati na paridevati na urattāḷiṃ kandati na sammohaṃ āpajjati. Tassa kho esā, aggivessana, uppannāpi sukhā vedanā cittaṃ na pariyādāya tiṭṭhati bhāvitattā kāyassa, uppannāpi dukkhā vedanā cittaṃ na pariyādāya tiṭṭhati bhāvitattā cittassa. Yassa kassaci, aggivessana, evaṃ ubhatopakkhaṃ uppannāpi sukhā vedanā cittaṃ na pariyādāya tiṭṭhati bhāvitattā kāyassa, uppannāpi dukkhā vedanā cittaṃ na pariyādāya tiṭṭhati bhāvitattā cittassa. Evaṃ kho, aggivessana, bhāvitakāyo ca hoti bhāvitacitto cā”ti.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
1235\. 𑀓𑀣𑀜𑁆𑀘, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀘? 𑀇𑀥, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀲𑀼𑀢𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸. 𑀲𑁄 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀨𑀼𑀝𑁆𑀞𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀦 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀲𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀻 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺, 𑀦 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀲𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀺𑀢𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀲𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀦𑀺𑀭𑀼𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀦𑀺𑀭𑁄𑀥𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸. 𑀲𑁄 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸𑀬 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀨𑀼𑀝𑁆𑀞𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀦 𑀲𑁄𑀘𑀢𑀺 𑀦 𑀓𑀺𑀮𑀫𑀢𑀺 𑀦 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀤𑁂𑀯𑀢𑀺 𑀦 𑀉𑀭𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀍𑀇𑀁 𑀓𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀢𑀺 𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄𑀳𑀁 𑀆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀔𑁄 𑀏𑀲𑀸, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲, 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀘𑀺, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀉𑀪𑀢𑁄𑀧𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀲, 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀔𑁄, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀘𑀸”𑀢𑀺.

## Renunciation

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
1236\. “I have confidence in Bho Gotama thus: Bho Gotama is developed in body and developed in mind.”

“Surely, Aggivessana, this speech of yours is insulting and insinuating[^1], nevertheless I will answer you. Since the time, Aggivessana, that I shaved off my hair and beard, put on saffron robes, and went forth from the home life into homelessness, it is not possible that arisen pleasant feeling could invade my mind and remain, or that arisen painful feeling could invade my mind and remain.”

1237\. “Perhaps there has not arisen for Bho Gotama such a pleasant feeling that, having arisen, it would invade the mind and remain; perhaps there has not arisen for Bho Gotama such a painful feeling that, having arisen, it would invade the mind and remain.”

[^1]: It is strange that the Buddha is offended by Saccaka's seemingly innocuous, and some might say even respectful words. Perhaps the Buddha was detecting sarcasm in his voice.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
1236\. “Evaṃ pasanno ahaṃ bhoto gotamassa. Bhavañhi gotamo bhāvitakāyo ca hoti bhāvitacitto cā”ti. “Addhā kho te ayaṃ, aggivessana, āsajja upanīya vācā bhāsitā, api ca te ahaṃ byākarissāmi. Yato kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajito, taṃ vata me uppannā vā sukhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, uppannā vā dukkhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassatīti netaṃ ṭhānaṃ vijjatī”ti.

1237\. “Na hi nūna bhoto gotamassa uppajjati tathārūpā sukhā vedanā yathārūpā uppannā sukhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭheyya; na hi nūna bhoto gotamassa uppajjati tathārūpā dukkhā vedanā yathārūpā uppannā dukkhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭheyyā”ti.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
1236\. “𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀧𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁 𑀪𑁄𑀢𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲. 𑀪𑀯𑀜𑁆𑀳𑀺 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫𑁄 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀘 𑀳𑁄𑀢𑀺 𑀪𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀘𑀸”𑀢𑀺. “𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸 𑀔𑁄 𑀢𑁂 𑀅𑀬𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀆𑀲𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀉𑀧𑀦𑀻𑀬 𑀯𑀸𑀘𑀸 𑀪𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀢𑀸, 𑀅𑀧𑀺 𑀘 𑀢𑁂 𑀅𑀳𑀁 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀓𑀭𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺. 𑀬𑀢𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀓𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀁 𑀑𑀳𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀬𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀅𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸𑀤𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀚𑀺𑀢𑁄, 𑀢𑀁 𑀯𑀢 𑀫𑁂 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀞𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀯𑀸 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀞𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀦𑁂𑀢𑀁 𑀞𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀻”𑀢𑀺.

1237\. “𑀦 𑀳𑀺 𑀦𑀽𑀦 𑀪𑁄𑀢𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺 𑀢𑀣𑀸𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬; 𑀦 𑀳𑀺 𑀦𑀽𑀦 𑀪𑁄𑀢𑁄 𑀕𑁄𑀢𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀢𑀺 𑀢𑀣𑀸𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀸 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀤𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀸 𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀸 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀢𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸”𑀢𑀺.

since | indeed | I | Aggivessana | hair and beard | having shaved off | ochre | robes | having put on | from household life | to homeless state | gone forth | that | indeed | my | arisen | or | pleasant | feeling | the mind | invading | will remain | arisen | or | painful | feeling | the mind | invading | will remain | thus | not | this | possibility | exists | (end quote)  
Since the time, Aggivessana, that I shaved off my hair and beard, put on saffron robes, and went forth from the home life into homelessness, it is not possible that arisen pleasant feeling could invade my mind and remain, or that arisen painful feeling could invade my mind and remain.”

1237.1 “Na hi nūna bhoto gotamassa uppajjati tathārūpā sukhā vedanā yathārūpā uppannā sukhā vedanā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭheyya;

## Āḷāra Kālāma

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
1238\. “Why should it not be, Aggivessana? Here, Aggivessana, before my `sambodhi` (full awakening), while still a Bodhisatta, not fully awakened, this thought occurred to me:

> ‘Household life is confining, a dusty path. Going forth is the open air. It is not easy for one living in a house to lead the `brahmacariya` (pure life) that is utterly perfect, utterly pure, like a polished shell. What if I, having shaved off my hair and beard, and having put on saffron robes, were to go forth from the home life into homelessness?’

So, Aggivessana, at a later time, while I was still young, with jet-black hair, endowed with the prime of youth, in the first stage of life — though my unwilling mother and father wept with tear-filled faces — having shaved off my hair and beard, and having put on saffron robes, I went forth from the home life into homelessness. Having thus gone forth, seeking what is wholesome, searching for the unsurpassed state of supreme peace, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was. Having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma:

> ‘I wish, Āvuso Kālāma, to lead the pure life in this Dhamma and Discipline.’

When this was said, Aggivessana, Āḷāra Kālāma said this to me:

> ‘May the venerable one stay; such is this Dhamma that a wise person, in no long time, having realised his own teacher's doctrine for himself through `abhiññā` (experiential understanding), may enter and dwell in it.’

So, Aggivessana, quickly, in no long time, I learned that Dhamma. So, Aggivessana, merely by lip-service, by mere verbal declaration, I would speak of the doctrine of knowledge and the doctrine of elders, and I would claim, ‘I know, I see,’ and so would others. Then, Aggivessana, this thought occurred to me:

> ‘It is not by mere faith alone that Āḷāra Kālāma declares this Dhamma as something he enters and dwells in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding; surely Āḷāra Kālāma dwells in this Dhamma knowing and seeing it.’

1239\. Then, Aggivessana, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was; having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma:

> ‘To what extent, Āvuso Kālāma, do you declare this Dhamma as something you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding?’

When this was said, Aggivessana, Āḷāra Kālāma declared the `ākiñcaññāyatana` (base of nothingness). Then, Aggivessana, this thought occurred to me:

> ‘It is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `saddhā` (faith); I also have faith; it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `vīriya` (energy); I also have energy; it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `sati` (awareness); I also have awareness; it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `samādhi` (focus); I also have focus; it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `paññā` (wisdom); I also have wisdom. What if I were to strive for the realisation of that Dhamma which Āḷāra Kālāma declares he enters and dwells in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding?’

So, Aggivessana, quickly, in no long time, having realised that Dhamma for myself through experiential understanding, I entered and dwelt in it.

1240\. Then, Aggivessana, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was; having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma:

> ‘Is it to this extent, Āvuso Kālāma, that you declare this Dhamma, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding and entered upon it?’

> ‘To this extent indeed, Āvuso, do I declare this Dhamma, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it.’

> ‘Indeed I too, Āvuso, to this extent enter and dwell in this Dhamma, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding.’

> ‘It is a gain for us, Āvuso, it is a great gain for us, that we see such a venerable one as a companion in the pure life. Thus, the Dhamma that I declare, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it, that Dhamma you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding. The Dhamma that you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding, that Dhamma I declare, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it. Thus, the Dhamma that I know, that Dhamma you know; the Dhamma that you know, that Dhamma I know. Thus, as I am, so are you; as you are, so am I. Come now, Āvuso, let us both, being present, lead this company.’

Thus, Aggivessana, Āḷāra Kālāma, being my teacher, placed me, his own pupil, on the same level as himself, and honoured me with great honour. Then, Aggivessana, this thought occurred to me:

> ‘This Dhamma does not lead to giving up, nor to dispassion, nor to cessation, nor to calm, nor to experiential understanding, nor to full awakening, nor to `nibbāna` (extinguishment); it leads only to reappearance in the base of nothingness.’

So, Aggivessana, not valuing that Dhamma, having given up that Dhamma, I departed.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
1238\. “Kiñhi no siyā, aggivessana? Idha me, aggivessana, pubbeva sambodhā anabhisambuddhassa bodhisattasseva sato etadahosi—  ‘sambādho gharāvāso rajāpatho, abbhokāso pabbajjā. Nayidaṃ sukaraṃ agāraṃ ajjhāvasatā ekantaparipuṇṇaṃ ekantaparisuddhaṃ saṅkhalikhitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ carituṃ. Yannūnāhaṃ kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajeyyan’ti. So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, aparena samayena daharova samāno, susukāḷakeso bhadrena yobbanena samannāgato paṭhamena vayasā, akāmakānaṃ mātāpitūnaṃ assumukhānaṃ rudantānaṃ, kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajiṃ. So evaṃ pabbajito samāno kiṃkusalagavesī anuttaraṃ santivarapadaṃ pariyesamāno yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘icchāmahaṃ, āvuso kālāma, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye brahmacariyaṃ caritun’ti. Evaṃ vutte, aggivessana, āḷāro kālāmo maṃ etadavoca—  ‘viharatāyasmā, tādiso ayaṃ dhammo yattha viññū puriso nacirasseva sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihareyyā’ti. So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, nacirasseva khippameva taṃ dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇiṃ. So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, tāvatakeneva oṭṭhapahatamattena lapitalāpanamattena ñāṇavādañca vadāmi theravādañca, ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti ca paṭijānāmi, ahañceva aññe ca. Tassa mayhaṃ, aggivessana, etadahosi—  ‘na kho āḷāro kālāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ kevalaṃ saddhāmattakena sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedeti, addhā āḷāro kālāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ jānaṃ passaṃ viharatī’ti.

1239\. Atha khvāhaṃ, aggivessana, yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘kittāvatā no, āvuso kālāma, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedesī’ti? Evaṃ vutte, aggivessana, āḷāro kālāmo ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ pavedesi. Tassa mayhaṃ, aggivessana, etadahosi—  ‘na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi saddhā, mayhampatthi saddhā; na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi vīriyaṃ, mayhampatthi vīriyaṃ; na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi sati, mayhampatthi sati; na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi samādhi, mayhampatthi samādhi; na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi paññā, mayhampatthi paññā; yannūnāhaṃ yaṃ dhammaṃ āḷāro kālāmo sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedeti tassa dhammassa sacchikiriyāya padaheyyan’ti. So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, nacirasseva khippameva taṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihāsiṃ.

1240\. Atha khvāhaṃ, aggivessana, yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘ettāvatā no, āvuso kālāma, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedesī’ti? ‘Ettāvatā kho ahaṃ, āvuso, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemī’ti. ‘Ahampi kho, āvuso, ettāvatā imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmī’ti. ‘Lābhā no, āvuso, suladdhaṃ no, āvuso, ye mayaṃ āyasmantaṃ tādisaṃ sabrahmacāriṃ passāma. Iti yāhaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemi taṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharasi; yaṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharasi tamahaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemi. Iti yāhaṃ dhammaṃ jānāmi taṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ jānāsi; yaṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ jānāsi tamahaṃ dhammaṃ jānāmi. Iti yādiso ahaṃ tādiso tuvaṃ, yādiso tuvaṃ tādiso ahaṃ. Ehi dāni, āvuso, ubhova santā imaṃ gaṇaṃ pariharāmā’ti. Iti kho, aggivessana, āḷāro kālāmo ācariyo me samāno attano antevāsiṃ maṃ samānaṃ attanā samasamaṃ ṭhapesi, uḷārāya ca maṃ pūjāya pūjesi. Tassa mayhaṃ, aggivessana, etadahosi—  ‘nāyaṃ dhammo nibbidāya na virāgāya na nirodhāya na upasamāya na abhiññāya na sambodhāya na nibbānāya saṃvattati, yāvadeva ākiñcaññāyatanūpapattiyā’ti. So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, taṃ dhammaṃ analaṅkaritvā tasmā dhammā nibbijja apakkamiṃ.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
1238\. “𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀳𑀺 𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀺𑀬𑀸, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦? 𑀇𑀥 𑀫𑁂, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑁂𑀯 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑁄𑀥𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀪𑀺𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁄𑀥𑀺𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀲𑀢𑁄 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀥𑁄 𑀖𑀭𑀸𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁄 𑀭𑀚𑀸𑀧𑀣𑁄, 𑀅𑀩𑁆𑀪𑁄𑀓𑀸𑀲𑁄 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀸. 𑀦𑀬𑀺𑀤𑀁 𑀲𑀼𑀓𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀸𑀯𑀲𑀢𑀸 𑀏𑀓𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀧𑀼𑀡𑁆𑀡𑀁 𑀏𑀓𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀲𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀁 𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀔𑀮𑀺𑀔𑀺𑀢𑀁 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀢𑀼𑀁. 𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀽𑀦𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀓𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀁 𑀑𑀳𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀬𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀅𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸𑀤𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀚𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀅𑀧𑀭𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀤𑀳𑀭𑁄𑀯 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄, 𑀲𑀼𑀲𑀼𑀓𑀸𑀍𑀅𑀓𑁂𑀲𑁄 𑀪𑀤𑁆𑀭𑁂𑀦 𑀬𑁄𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀦𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀕𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀞𑀫𑁂𑀦 𑀯𑀬𑀲𑀸, 𑀅𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀓𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀫𑀸𑀢𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀢𑀽𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀭𑀼𑀤𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀁, 𑀓𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀁 𑀑𑀳𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀬𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀅𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸𑀤𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀚𑀺𑀁. 𑀲𑁄 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀚𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀓𑀺𑀁𑀓𑀼𑀲𑀮𑀕𑀯𑁂𑀲𑀻 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀯𑀭𑀧𑀤𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀁 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—  ‘𑀇𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸𑀫𑀳𑀁, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫, 𑀇𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀺𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀯𑀺𑀦𑀬𑁂 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀢𑀼𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺. 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—  ‘𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸, 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀬𑀢𑁆𑀣 𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀽 𑀧𑀼𑀭𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀲𑀓𑀁 𑀆𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑀺𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀧𑀼𑀡𑀺𑀁. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀓𑁂𑀦𑁂𑀯 𑀑𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀳𑀢𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀮𑀧𑀺𑀢𑀮𑀸𑀧𑀦𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀜𑀸𑀡𑀯𑀸𑀤𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀯𑀤𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀣𑁂𑀭𑀯𑀸𑀤𑀜𑁆𑀘, ‘𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀻’𑀢𑀺 𑀘 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺, 𑀅𑀳𑀜𑁆𑀘𑁂𑀯 𑀅𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂 𑀘. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀓𑁂𑀯𑀮𑀁 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀓𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀻’𑀢𑀺.

1239\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀁 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—  ‘𑀓𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫, 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀻’𑀢𑀺? 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀆𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀯𑀻𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀯𑀻𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀢𑀺; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀥𑀺, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀥𑀺; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸; 𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀽𑀦𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀢𑀺 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀺𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀤𑀳𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑀺𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁.

1240\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀁 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—  ‘𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫, 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀻’𑀢𑀺? ‘𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀫𑀻’𑀢𑀺. ‘𑀅𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀔𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻’𑀢𑀺. ‘𑀮𑀸𑀪𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀲𑀼𑀮𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀁 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀬𑁂 𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑀁 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀁 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀫𑀺 𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀲𑀺; 𑀬𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀲𑀺 𑀢𑀫𑀳𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀫𑀺. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀺; 𑀬𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀺 𑀢𑀫𑀳𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀢𑀼𑀯𑀁, 𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀢𑀼𑀯𑀁 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁. 𑀏𑀳𑀺 𑀤𑀸𑀦𑀺, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀉𑀪𑁄𑀯 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀕𑀡𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀔𑁄, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀆𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁄 𑀫𑁂 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀯𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁 𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀫𑀲𑀫𑀁 𑀞𑀧𑁂𑀲𑀺, 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀬 𑀘 𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀽𑀚𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀽𑀚𑁂𑀲𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀯𑀺𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀦𑀺𑀭𑁄𑀥𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑁄𑀥𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀲𑀁𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀢𑀺, 𑀬𑀸𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀯 𑀆𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀽𑀧𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀺𑀯𑁂𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦, 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀮𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀭𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀺𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀅𑀧𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁.
</TabItem>

1238.1 “Kiñhi no siyā, aggivessana?  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(kiṃ) ⏏️(hi) ⏏️(no) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(atthi) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana)  
why | indeed | not | should it be | Aggivessana  
“Why should it not be, Aggivessana?

1238.2 Idha me, aggivessana, pubbeva sambodhā anabhisambuddhassa bodhisattasseva sato etadahosi—  ‘sambādho gharāvāso rajāpatho, abbhokāso pabbajjā.  
⏏️(idha) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) ⏏️(pubbe) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑤(sambodha) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)🆎(na+abhisambuddha) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(bodhi+satta) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(anta)(santa) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(hoti) 🚹⨀①(sambādha) 🚹⨀①🆎(ghara+āvāsa) 🚹⨀①🆎(rajas+patha) 🚹⨀①(abbhokāsa) 🚺⨀①(pabbajjā)  
here | to me | Aggivessana | before | just | full awakening | not fully awakened | a Bodhisatta | still | being | this | occurred | confining | household life | a dusty path | the open air | going forth  
Here, Aggivessana, before my `sambodhi` (full awakening), while still a Bodhisatta, not fully awakened, this thought occurred to me: ‘Household life is confining, a dusty path. Going forth is the open air.

1238.3 Nayidaṃ sukaraṃ agāraṃ ajjhāvasatā ekantaparipuṇṇaṃ ekantaparisuddhaṃ saṅkhalikhitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ carituṃ.  
⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ima) 🚻⨀①🆎(su+kara) 🚻⨀②(agāra) 🚹⨀③🔽(anta)(ajjhāvasanta) 🚻⨀②🆎(ekanta+paripuṇṇa) 🚻⨀②🆎(ekanta+parisuddha) 🚻⨀②🆎(saṅkha+likhita) 🚻⨀②🆎(brahma+cariya) 🔽(tuṃ)(carati)  
not | this | easy to do | domestic living | by one domestically settled | completely perfect | completely pure | flawless | the pure life | to live  
It is not easy for one living in a house to lead the `brahmacariya` (pure life) that is utterly perfect, utterly pure, like a polished shell.

1238.4 Yannūnāhaṃ kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajeyyan’ti.  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ya) ⏏️(nūna) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚻⨀②🆎(kesa+massu) 🔽(tvā)(ohāreti) 🚻⨂②(kāsāya) 🚻⨂②(vattha) 🔽(tvā)(acchādeti) 🚻⨀⑤(agāra) 🚻⨀②🆎(na+agāriya) 🔵⨀👆⏯(pabbajati) ⏏️(iti)  
what | if | I | hair and beard | having shaved off | ochre | robes | having put on | from household life | to homeless state | renounce | (end quote)  
What if I, having shaved off my hair and beard, and having put on saffron robes, were to go forth from the home life into homelessness?’

1238.5 So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, aparena samayena daharova samāno, susukāḷakeso bhadrena yobbanena samannāgato paṭhamena vayasā, akāmakānaṃ mātāpitūnaṃ assumukhānaṃ rudantānaṃ, kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajiṃ.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(apara) 🚹⨀③(samaya) 🚹⨀①(dahara) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(samāna) 🚹⨀①🆎(susu+kāḷa+kesa) 🚻⨀③(bhadra) 🚻⨀③(yobbana) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(samannāgata) 🚹⨀③(paṭhama) 🚹⨀③(vayas) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(na+kāmaka) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(mātar+pitar) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(assu+mukha) 🚹⨂⑥🔽(anta)(rudanta) 🚻⨀②🆎(kesa+massu) 🔽(tvā)(ohāreti) 🚻⨂②(kāsāya) 🚻⨂②(vattha) 🔽(tvā)(acchādeti) 🚻⨀⑤(agāra) 🚻⨀②🆎(na+agāriya) 🟢⨀👆⏮(pabbajati)  
that | indeed | I | Aggivessana | at a later | time | young | still | being | with jet-black hair | with the prime | of youth | endowed with | in the first | stage of life | of the unwilling | mother and father | with tear-filled faces | weeping | hair and beard | having shaved off | ochre | robes | having put on | from household life | to homeless state | renounced  
So, Aggivessana, at a later time, while I was still young, with jet-black hair, endowed with the prime of youth, in the first stage of life — though my unwilling mother and father wept with tear-filled faces — having shaved off my hair and beard, and having put on saffron robes, I went forth from the home life into homelessness.

1238.6 So evaṃ pabbajito samāno kiṃkusalagavesī anuttaraṃ santivarapadaṃ pariyesamāno yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ;  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) ⏏️(evaṃ) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(pabbajita) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(samāna) 🚹⨀①🆎(kiṃ+kusala+gavesī) 🚻⨀②🆎(na+uttara) 🚻⨀②🆎(santi+vara+pada) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(pariyesamāna) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ya) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ta) 🟢⨀👆⏮(upasaṅkamati)  
that | thus | gone forth | being | seeking what is wholesome | unsurpassed | state of supreme peace | searching for | by where | Āḷāra | Kālāma | by there | I approached  
Having thus gone forth, seeking what is wholesome, searching for the unsurpassed state of supreme peace, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was.

1238.7 upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘icchāmahaṃ, āvuso kālāma, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye brahmacariyaṃ caritun’ti.  
🔽(tvā)(upasaṅkamati) 🚹⨀②(āḷāra) 🚹⨀②(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(eta) 🟢⨀👆⏮(vadati) 🟢⨀👆▶️(icchati) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) ⏏️(āvuso) 🚹⨀⓪(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(ima) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(dhamma+vinaya) 🚻⨀②🆎(brahma+cariya) 🔽(tuṃ)(carati) ⏏️(iti)  
having approached | Āḷāra | Kālāma | this | I said | I wish | I | Āvuso | Kālāma | in this | Dhamma and Discipline | the pure life | to lead | (end quote)  
Having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma: ‘I wish, Āvuso Kālāma, to lead the pure life in this Dhamma and Discipline.’

1238.8 Evaṃ vutte, aggivessana, āḷāro kālāmo maṃ etadavoca—  ‘viharatāyasmā, tādiso ayaṃ dhammo yattha viññū puriso nacirasseva sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihareyyā’ti.  
⏏️(evaṃ) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(ta)(vutta) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) *️⃣👆⚧⨀②(ahaṃ) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(vadati) 🟢⨀🤟⏹(viharati) 🚹⨀①(āyasmant) 🚹⨀①(tādisa) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ima) 🚹⨀①(dhamma) ⏏️(yattha) 🚹⨀①(viññū) 🚹⨀①(purisa) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(cirassa) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀②(saka) 🚹⨀②(ācariyaka) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(viharati) ⏏️(iti)  
thus | said | Aggivessana | Āḷāra | Kālāma | me | this | said | may stay | the venerable one | such | this | Dhamma | in which | wise | person | not | after a long time | just | his own | teacher's doctrine | for himself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | may dwell | (end quote)  
When this was said, Aggivessana, Āḷāra Kālāma said this to me: ‘May the venerable one stay; such is this Dhamma that a wise person, in no long time, having realised his own teacher's doctrine for himself through `abhiññā` (experiential understanding), may enter and dwell in it.’

1238.9 So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, nacirasseva khippameva taṃ dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇiṃ.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(cirassa) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀②(khippa) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ta) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🟢⨀👆⏮(pariyāpuṇāti)  
that | indeed | I | Aggivessana | not | after a long time | just | quickly | just | that | Dhamma | I learned  
So, Aggivessana, quickly, in no long time, I learned that Dhamma.

1238.10 So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, tāvatakeneva oṭṭhapahatamattena lapitalāpanamattena ñāṇavādañca vadāmi theravādañca, ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti ca paṭijānāmi, ahañceva aññe ca.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚻⨀③(tāvataka) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀③🆎(oṭṭha+pahata+matta) 🚻⨀③🆎(lapita+lāpana+matta) 🚹⨀②🆎(ñāṇa+vāda) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀👆▶️(vadati) 🚹⨀②🆎(thera+vāda) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀👆▶️(jānāti) 🟢⨀👆▶️(passati) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀👆▶️(paṭijānāti) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(añña) ⏏️(ca)  
that | indeed | I | Aggivessana | by just so much | merely | by mere lip-service | by mere verbal declaration | the doctrine of knowledge | and | I speak of | the doctrine of elders | and | I know | I see | (end quote) | and | I claim | I | and | indeed | others | and  
So, Aggivessana, merely by lip-service, by mere verbal declaration, I would speak of the doctrine of knowledge and the doctrine of elders, and I would claim, ‘I know, I see,’ and so would others.

1238.11 Tassa mayhaṃ, aggivessana, etadahosi—  ‘na kho āḷāro kālāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ kevalaṃ saddhāmattakena sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedeti, addhā āḷāro kālāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ jānaṃ passaṃ viharatī’ti.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀④(ta) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(hoti) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ima) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🚻⨀②(kevala) 🚻⨀③🆎(saddhā+mattaka) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟢⨀👆▶️(viharati) ⏏️(iti) 🟠⨀🤟▶️(pavedeti) ⏏️(addhā) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ima) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🚹⨀①🔽(anta)(jānanta) 🚹⨀①🔽(anta)(passanta) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(viharati) ⏏️(iti)  
to that | to me | Aggivessana | this | occurred | not | indeed | Āḷāra | Kālāma | this | Dhamma | only | by mere faith | for himself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | I dwell | (end quote) | declares | surely | Āḷāra | Kālāma | this | Dhamma | knowing | seeing | dwells | (end quote)  
Then, Aggivessana, this thought occurred to me: ‘It is not by mere faith alone that Āḷāra Kālāma declares this Dhamma as something he enters and dwells in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding; surely Āḷāra Kālāma dwells in this Dhamma knowing and seeing it.’

1239.1 Atha khvāhaṃ, aggivessana, yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ;  
⏏️(atha) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ya) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ta) 🟢⨀👆⏮(upasaṅkamati)  
then | indeed | I | Aggivessana | by where | Āḷāra | Kālāma | by there | I approached  
Then, Aggivessana, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was;

1239.2 upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘kittāvatā no, āvuso kālāma, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedesī’ti?  
🔽(tvā)(upasaṅkamati) 🚹⨀②(āḷāra) 🚹⨀②(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(eta) 🟢⨀👆⏮(vadati) ⏏️(kittāvatā) ⏏️(no) ⏏️(āvuso) 🚹⨀⓪(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ima) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟢⨀👆▶️(viharati) ⏏️(iti) 🟠⨀🤘▶️(pavedeti) ⏏️(iti)  
having approached | Āḷāra | Kālāma | this | I said | to what extent | then | Āvuso | Kālāma | this | Dhamma | for yourself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | I dwell | (end quote) | you declare | (end quote)  
having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma: ‘To what extent, Āvuso Kālāma, do you declare this Dhamma as something you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding?’

1239.3 Evaṃ vutte, aggivessana, āḷāro kālāmo ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ pavedesi.  
⏏️(evaṃ) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(ta)(vutta) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) 🚻⨀②🆎(ākiñcañña+āyatana) 🟠⨀🤟⏮(pavedeti)  
thus | said | Aggivessana | Āḷāra | Kālāma | the base of nothingness | declared  
When this was said, Aggivessana, Āḷāra Kālāma declared the `ākiñcaññāyatana` (base of nothingness).

1239.4 Tassa mayhaṃ, aggivessana, etadahosi—  ‘na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi saddhā, mayhampatthi saddhā;  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀④(ta) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(hoti) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀⑥(āḷāra) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑥(kālāma) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚺⨀①(saddhā) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(ahaṃ) ⏏️(api) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚺⨀①(saddhā)  
to that | to me | Aggivessana | this | occurred | not | indeed | of Āḷāra | only | of Kālāma | there is | faith | of me | too | there is | faith  
Then, Aggivessana, this thought occurred to me: ‘It is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `saddhā` (faith); I also have faith;

1239.5 na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi vīriyaṃ, mayhampatthi vīriyaṃ;  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀⑥(āḷāra) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑥(kālāma) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚻⨀①(vīriya) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(ahaṃ) ⏏️(api) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚻⨀①(vīriya)  
not | indeed | of Āḷāra | only | of Kālāma | there is | energy | of me | too | there is | energy  
it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `vīriya` (energy); I also have energy;

1239.6 na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi sati, mayhampatthi sati;  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀⑥(āḷāra) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑥(kālāma) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚺⨀①(sati) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(ahaṃ) ⏏️(api) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚺⨀①(sati)  
not | indeed | of Āḷāra | only | of Kālāma | there is | awareness | of me | too | there is | awareness  
it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `sati` (awareness); I also have awareness;

1239.7 na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi samādhi, mayhampatthi samādhi;  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀⑥(āḷāra) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑥(kālāma) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚹⨀①(samādhi) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(ahaṃ) ⏏️(api) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚹⨀①(samādhi)  
not | indeed | of Āḷāra | only | of Kālāma | there is | focus | of me | too | there is | focus  
it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `samādhi` (focus); I also have focus;

1239.8 na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi paññā, mayhampatthi paññā;  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀⑥(āḷāra) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑥(kālāma) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚺⨀①(paññā) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(ahaṃ) ⏏️(api) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(atthi) 🚺⨀①(paññā)  
not | indeed | of Āḷāra | only | of Kālāma | there is | wisdom | of me | too | there is | wisdom  
it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `paññā` (wisdom); I also have wisdom.

1239.9 yannūnāhaṃ yaṃ dhammaṃ āḷāro kālāmo sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedeti tassa dhammassa sacchikiriyāya padaheyyan’ti.  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ya) ⏏️(nūna) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ya) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟢⨀👆▶️(viharati) ⏏️(iti) 🟠⨀🤟▶️(pavedeti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(ta) 🚹⨀⑥(dhamma) 🚺⨀④🆎(sacchi+kiriyā) 🔵⨀👆⏯(padahati) ⏏️(iti)  
what | if | I | which | Dhamma | Āḷāra | Kālāma | for himself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | I dwell | (end quote) | declares | of that | Dhamma | for the realisation | I were to strive | (end quote)  
What if I were to strive for the realisation of that Dhamma which Āḷāra Kālāma declares he enters and dwells in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding?’

1239.10 So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, nacirasseva khippameva taṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihāsiṃ.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(cirassa) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀②(khippa) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ta) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟢⨀👆⏮(viharati)  
that | indeed | I | Aggivessana | not | after a long time | just | quickly | just | that | Dhamma | for myself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | I dwelt  
So, Aggivessana, quickly, in no long time, having realised that Dhamma for myself through experiential understanding, I entered and dwelt in it.

1240.1 Atha khvāhaṃ, aggivessana, yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ;  
⏏️(atha) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ya) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(ta) 🟢⨀👆⏮(upasaṅkamati)  
then | indeed | I | Aggivessana | by where | Āḷāra | Kālāma | by there | I approached  
Then, Aggivessana, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was;

1240.2 upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘ettāvatā no, āvuso kālāma, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedesī’ti?  
🔽(tvā)(upasaṅkamati) 🚹⨀②(āḷāra) 🚹⨀②(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(eta) 🟢⨀👆⏮(vadati) ⏏️(ettāvatā) ⏏️(no) ⏏️(āvuso) 🚹⨀⓪(kālāma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ima) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟠⨀🤘▶️(pavedeti) ⏏️(iti)  
having approached | Āḷāra | Kālāma | this | I said | to this extent | then | Āvuso | Kālāma | this | Dhamma | for yourself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | you declare | (end quote)  
having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma: ‘Is it to this extent, Āvuso Kālāma, that you declare this Dhamma, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding and entered upon it?’

1240.3 ‘Ettāvatā kho ahaṃ, āvuso, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemī’ti.  
⏏️(ettāvatā) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) ⏏️(āvuso) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ima) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟠⨀👆▶️(pavedeti) ⏏️(iti)  
to this extent | indeed | I | Āvuso | this | Dhamma | for myself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | I declare | (end quote)  
‘To this extent indeed, Āvuso, do I declare this Dhamma, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it.’

1240.4 ‘Ahampi kho, āvuso, ettāvatā imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmī’ti.  
*️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(kho) ⏏️(āvuso) ⏏️(ettāvatā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ima) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟢⨀👆▶️(viharati) ⏏️(iti)  
I | too | indeed | Āvuso | to this extent | this | Dhamma | for myself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | I dwell in | (end quote)  
‘Indeed I too, Āvuso, to this extent enter and dwell in this Dhamma, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding.’

1240.5 ‘Lābhā no, āvuso, suladdhaṃ no, āvuso, ye mayaṃ āyasmantaṃ tādisaṃ sabrahmacāriṃ passāma.  
⏏️(lābhā) *️⃣👆⚧⨂④(ahaṃ) ⏏️(āvuso) ⏏️(suladdhaṃ) *️⃣👆⚧⨂④(ahaṃ) ⏏️(āvuso) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ya) *️⃣👆⚧⨂①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀②(āyasmant) 🚹⨀②(tādisa) 🚹⨀②🆎(sa+brahmacārī) 🟢⨂👆▶️(passati)  
it is a gain | for us | Āvuso | it is a great gain | for us | Āvuso | who | we | the venerable one | such | a companion in the pure life | we see  
‘It is a gain for us, Āvuso, it is a great gain for us, that we see such a venerable one as a companion in the pure life.

1240.6 Iti yāhaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemi taṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharasi;  
⏏️(iti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ya) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟠⨀👆▶️(pavedeti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ta) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(tvaṃ) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(viharati)  
thus | which | I | Dhamma | for myself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | I declare | that | you | Dhamma | for yourself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | you dwell in  
Thus, the Dhamma that I declare, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it, that Dhamma you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding.

1240.7 yaṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharasi tamahaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemi.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ya) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(tvaṃ) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(viharati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ta) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) ⏏️(sayaṃ) 🚺⨀③(abhiññā) 🔽(tvā)(sacchikaroti) 🔽(ya)(upasampajjati) 🟠⨀👆▶️(pavedeti)  
which | you | Dhamma | for yourself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | you dwell in | that | I | Dhamma | for myself | through experiential understanding | having realised | entering | I declare  
The Dhamma that you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding, that Dhamma I declare, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it.

1240.8 Iti yāhaṃ dhammaṃ jānāmi taṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ jānāsi;  
⏏️(iti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ya) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🟢⨀👆▶️(jānāti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ta) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(tvaṃ) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(jānāti)  
thus | which | I | Dhamma | I know | that | you | Dhamma | you know  
Thus, the Dhamma that I know, that Dhamma you know;

1240.9 yaṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ jānāsi tamahaṃ dhammaṃ jānāmi.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ya) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(tvaṃ) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(jānāti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ta) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🟢⨀👆▶️(jānāti)  
which | you | Dhamma | you know | that | I | Dhamma | I know  
the Dhamma that you know, that Dhamma I know.

1240.10 Iti yādiso ahaṃ tādiso tuvaṃ, yādiso tuvaṃ tādiso ahaṃ.  
⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀①(yādisa) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀①(tādisa) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(tvaṃ) 🚹⨀①(yādisa) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(tvaṃ) 🚹⨀①(tādisa) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ)  
thus | of whatever kind | I | of such kind | you | of whatever kind | you | of such kind | I  
Thus, as I am, so are you; as you are, so am I.

1240.11 Ehi dāni, āvuso, ubhova santā imaṃ gaṇaṃ pariharāmā’ti.  
🟢⨀🤘⏹(eti) ⏏️(dāni) ⏏️(āvuso) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(ubha) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨂①🔽(anta)(santa) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ima) 🚹⨀②(gaṇa) 🟢⨂👆⏹(pariharati) ⏏️(iti)  
come | now | Āvuso | both | just | being present | this | company | let us lead | (end quote)  
Come now, Āvuso, let us both, being present, lead this company.’

1240.12 Iti kho, aggivessana, āḷāro kālāmo ācariyo me samāno attano antevāsiṃ maṃ samānaṃ attanā samasamaṃ ṭhapesi, uḷārāya ca maṃ pūjāya pūjesi.  
⏏️(iti) ⏏️(kho) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) 🚹⨀①(āḷāra) 🚹⨀①(kālāma) 🚹⨀①(ācariya) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(samāna) 🚹⨀⑥(atta) 🚹⨀②🆎(ante+vāsī) *️⃣👆⚧⨀②(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀②🔽(māna)(samāna) 🚹⨀③(atta) 🚹⨀②🆎(sama+sama) 🟠⨀🤟⏮(ṭhapeti) 🚺⨀③(uḷāra) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣👆⚧⨀②(ahaṃ) 🚺⨀③(pūjā) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(pūjeti)  
thus | indeed | Aggivessana | Āḷāra | Kālāma | teacher | my | being | his own | pupil | me | being | with himself | on the same level | placed | with great | and | me | honour | honoured  
Thus, Aggivessana, Āḷāra Kālāma, being my teacher, placed me, his own pupil, on the same level as himself, and honoured me with great honour.

1240.13 Tassa mayhaṃ, aggivessana, etadahosi—  ‘nāyaṃ dhammo nibbidāya na virāgāya na nirodhāya na upasamāya na abhiññāya na sambodhāya na nibbānāya saṃvattati, yāvadeva ākiñcaññāyatanūpapattiyā’ti.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀④(ta) *️⃣👆⚧⨀④(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(eta) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(hoti) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ima) 🚹⨀①(dhamma) 🚺⨀④(nibbidā) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀④(virāga) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀④(nirodha) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀④(upasama) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀④(abhiññā) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀④(sambodha) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀④(nibbāna) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(saṃvattati) ⏏️(yāva) ⏏️(eva) 🚺⨀④🆎(ākiñcañña+āyatana+upapatti) ⏏️(iti)  
to that | to me | Aggivessana | this | was | not | this | Dhamma | to giving up | nor | to detachment | nor | to cessation | nor | to calm | nor | to experiential understanding | nor | to full awakening | nor | to extinguishment | leads | as far as | only | to reappearance in the base of nothingness | (end quote)  
Then, Aggivessana, this thought occurred to me: ‘This Dhamma does not lead to giving up, nor to dispassion, nor to cessation, nor to calm, nor to experiential understanding, nor to full awakening, nor to `nibbāna` (extinguishment); it leads only to reappearance in the base of nothingness.’

1240.14 So kho ahaṃ, aggivessana, taṃ dhammaṃ analaṅkaritvā tasmā dhammā nibbijja apakkamiṃ.  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(ta) ⏏️(kho) *️⃣👆⚧⨀①(ahaṃ) 🚹⨀⓪(aggivessana) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ta) 🚹⨀②(dhamma) 🔽(tvā)🆎(na+alaṅkaroti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑤(ta) 🚹⨀⑤(dhamma) 🔽(ya)(nibbindati) 🟢⨀👆⏮(apakkamati)  
that | indeed | I | Aggivessana | that | Dhamma | not valuing | from that | Dhamma | having given up | I departed  
So, Aggivessana, not valuing that Dhamma, having given up that Dhamma, I departed.
